replace-cross Is the Judge Its Twin? Post-training makes a model's writing more predictable but barely moves its taste, as a judge, toward predictable writing

Authors: Arman Nik Khah, Arvin Bahreini

Abstract: Language models now routinely grade other language models. If post-training makes a model's writing more predictable, it may also teach the same model, as a judge, to reward predictable writing. We test this directly. We follow two open 7-billion-parameter model families, OLMo-2 and Zephyr, from their base models through supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and direct preference optimization (DPO), and measure every stage as a writer of short stories and as a judge of story pairs. As writers, the models drift as feared. Each family's trained model finds its stages' stories more familiar in the order base, SFT, DPO, in all ten prompts, and a model from the other family finds the trained stories 7.0 to 9.2 percent (OLMo-2) and 24 to 25 percent (Zephyr) less surprising per token. As judges, they barely move toward predictable writing. Asked which story is better, a question every judge passes a competence check on, no trained judge's preference for the more predictable story grows by as much as one point of pick probability at its estimate. A one-sided 95% upper bound on that growth is about 5 points on the widest pairs. Training instead makes judges more swayed by story length and by display order, and it breaks "more creative" as a question. Trained judges no longer reliably prefer a story to its own words in scrambled order. For these two families, the practical hazard in a small trained judge of creative writing lies in these measurable habits.

replace-cross PACT: End-to-End Learning of Human Pose, Contacts, and Forces from Video

Authors: Rikhat Akizhanov, Yangsong Zhang, Nikolai Kaliazin, Peter Wolf, Yoshihiko Nakamura, Pascal Fua, Fabio Pizzati, Ivan Laptev

Abstract: Human motion, environmental contacts, and interaction forces are governed by common physical laws, yet existing approaches typically separate visual pose reconstruction from contact and force estimation. This separation limits joint reasoning and can propagate errors between stages. We introduce PACT, an end-to-end model that jointly learns to estimate human pose, contacts and contact forces from monocular video. Our approach augments a human reconstruction foundation model with learnable contact-force tokens and a temporal transformer that integrates visual features with world-space motion. Joint prediction heads refine human poses and estimate contacts and forces, while physics-based supervision encourages consistency between the reconstructed motion and interaction forces. To address the scarcity of force annotations, we develop a data annotation pipeline that combines contact labeling with physics-based motion and force optimization, producing training supervision from synthetic and real-world videos. We also introduce a real-world climbing benchmark ForceWall with climbing videos and corresponding ground-truth contact forces obtained from the force sensors. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art contact and force estimation, outperforming staged reconstruction approaches and generalizing to interactions beyond the training distribution. These results support end-to-end joint learning as an effective approach to recovering human motion and physical interactions from video.

replace-cross FreeEvolve: Learning to Evolve Beyond Fixed Loops

Authors: Lecheng Kong, Like Hui, Nikos Kanakaris, Prithwish Jana, Sahika Genc, Narayanan Sadagopan

Abstract: Agent evolvers automate the design of the prompts, skills and workflows around language model agents, yet the optimization process they follow is still designed by hand: a fixed search loop decides how candidates are evaluated, which are kept and when the search stops. We propose FREEEVOLVE, which automates this process as well. An environment specifies the goal, target agent, evaluator, data and resource limits; within these limits, the evolver itself decides what to test, how much evidence to collect, which candidates to pursue and when to stop. These decisions follow an editable evolution skill, which we improve through meta-evolution by scoring each candidate skill on the fresh target agent it produces. The optimization process thus becomes a capability learned from experience rather than a loop engineered in advance. On tau3-bench, ARC-AGI-2, ARC-AGI-3 and Terminal-Bench 2.1, FREEEVOLVE controls the evolution campaign by itself, yet improves the primary held-out metric by 13.6 points on average and matches or exceeds hand-designed evolvers. The learned process keeps improving with experience: meta-evolved skills add 6.9 points over the seed skill on fresh target agents, demonstrating transferability across environments.

replace-cross ATTUNE: Agentic Throughput Tuning for LLM Training

Authors: Leo Fan, Hongyang Wang, Wenbo Chen, Jingguo Liu, Xian Li, Xuan Wang, Ruochen Wen, Yiming Yan, Hao Wu, Yichuan Li, Qian Qu, Zheshen Wang, Benoit Dumoulin

Abstract: The throughput of an LLM training job depends on its recipe: how the model is split across GPUs, how memory is traded for compute, and which kernels and communication overlaps it uses. The best recipe changes with every model, accelerator and framework version. Automated tuners search a fixed space of knobs and check only that a job runs, so they miss levers outside that space and can ship a configuration that silently changes training. ATTUNE is a coding agent that tunes any working recipe unattended within a GPU-hour budget. Guided by a skill of search rules, scripts and lessons from past tuning campaigns, it diagnoses each run and changes one lever at a time. A correctness gate outside the agent refuses any argument known to change the training objective. ATTUNE tunes Amazon's production post-training recipes on H100, H200 and B200 clusters, raising throughput by 1.7x on average. It beats the published throughput of four of NVIDIA's own benchmark recipes on NVIDIA's AutoModel framework by 6-17%. On models held out from its development, it improves the strongest public recipes most where they were written for another setting, by up to 2.6x on MiniMax-M2, and least where the vendor tuned them for the same hardware. Given the same GPU-hour budget, it outperforms existing automated tuners, including Bayesian optimization, random search and OptiCo, a recent LLM-agent tuner. Its gain on GLM-4.5 is more than four times the best alternative's. The same coding agent without ATTUNE's skill gains less and fails half its launches. We release ATTUNE at https://github.com/amazon-science/attune and OpenRecipes, a leaderboard of tuned and verified training recipes, at https://openrecipes.ai.

URLs: https://github.com/amazon-science/attune, https://openrecipes.ai.

replace-cross Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition for Building STRATA-S12: An Open SAR-Optical Corpus for Self-Supervised Learning

Authors: Christian Sanchez-Leon, Dongming Chen, Dongqi Wang

Abstract: Building a co-registered SAR-optical corpus for self-supervised Earth Observation is above all an acquisition problem, yet the main multi-modal datasets are collected opportunistically, with no per-stratum quota control, so the regions and seasons that cloud cover hits hardest end up under-sampled. We present Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition (DDSA), an algorithm that casts the construction of a Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 corpus as a constrained maximum-coverage problem over a land-cover-driven, hemisphere-corrected (class x season x region) stratification of target countries on all six inhabited continents, and solves it online: acquisition is ordered by marginal gain against a per-stratum deficit that the quality gates and a visual review of every accepted pair keep updating during the run. Three results follow from measuring the algorithm on its own product. First, stratum balance is a property of the deficit-driven ordering rather than of run length: replayed over all 2,928,370 real acquisition attempts, failures included, it populates all 192 strata within 0.05% of the attempts, against 4.03% for uniform round-robin effort and 20.5% for a random order. Second, whether a rejected attempt can be recovered is better predicted by how long its cause persists than by the type of defect: cloud rejections are recovered at eta = 18.8% against 41.4% for nodata, consistent with clouds persisting through a season. Third, the repair loop supplies 39.5% of the released pairs at no measurable quality cost, with 89.8% surviving manual review, but it adds yield rather than balance. The quality guarantee has a price: 2,928,370 acquisition attempts produced 740,746 released pairs. We release the corpus, STRATA-S12, with per-pair metadata sidecars aligned with the STAC and PDS4 standards and the FAIR principles, and a pre-registered linear probe confirms it is fit for self-supervised pre-training.

replace-cross An Adaptive-Horizon Graph Neural Operator for coupled surrogate morphodynamic modelling

Authors: Ying Cao, Yifan Yang, Junqiang Xia, Julia C. Mullarney, M\'ario J. Franca

Abstract: Morphodynamic simulations require repeated coupling between hydrodynamic solvers, sediment transport modules, and bed updating. For long duration, multi-scenario simulations on large unstructured meshes, such coupling is computationally demanding. This study proposes an Adaptive-Horizon Graph Neural Operator (AH-GNO) that formulates bed evolution as a graph operator learning problem from recent hydrodynamic and topographic states to predict future bed elevation increments. The model operates directly on the native unstructured meshes. The approach integrates area-weighted graph learning, historical state selection, and forecast horizon estimation, allowing the effective history length (K) and update interval (B) to vary with the evolving flow and bed state. Using Telemac2D/GAIA solutions as supervised numerical references, the framework is evaluated on the official Bump and Yen bend benchmarks and on a field-scale application (Waiho River, New Zealand). Results show that the AH-GNO reproduces the main patterns of bedform migration, bend-scale erosion and deposition, and localized bed adjustment. Across the three cases, the tested CPU-GPU deployment reduced post-training bed-update workflow time from 12~1204 s with GAIA to 0.08~3.34 s with AH-GNO. This improvement corresponds to deployment-specific accelerations of approximately 150~360 times relative to 4-core GAIA CPU configurations, and with 105~186 times gains for 20-core CPU configurations. These results suggest that, while retaining hydrodynamic feedback from TELEMAC-2D, a learned operator for bed updating can provide an efficient acceleration component for morphodynamic scenario analysis and rapid coupled simulation.

replace-cross WhatIf-Bench: Benchmarking Intervention-Aware Time Series Forecasting

Authors: Arjun Ashok, Mihir Parmar, Palash Goyal, Bhavana Dalvi Mishra, Chun-Liang Li, Irina Rish, Alexandre Drouin, Jinsung Yoon, Tomas Pfister

Abstract: Recent time series foundation models (TSFMs) can consume covariates in-context, extending zero-shot forecasting to problems driven by external factors. This enables scenario analysis: forecasting how a target responds to events that set covariates to chosen future values. Whether they can do this reliably is an open question. We introduce WhatIf-Bench, a benchmark measuring how well forecasters use covariates to forecast a target's response to future events. WhatIf-Bench is semi-synthetic: time series come from mechanistic simulators of data centers, power grids, road traffic, and water networks, with controlled covariate-target relationships grounded in each system. Each task presents a history of events intervening on covariates, then asks to forecast a future event that either replays seen values (Replay) or sets them to unseen ones (Novel). Varying the number of covariates, historical events, and event length yields 37,665 tasks. Evaluating TSFMs, frontier LLMs, and simple regression baselines, we find the LLMs reach the highest skill (improvement over a naive baseline), ahead of the best TSFMs. Every model forecasts well on Replay futures (48 to 85% skill) but drops to -7 to 50% on Novel futures, with the regression baselines falling the most. Skill on Novel futures also falls as events set more covariates jointly. More historical events raise skill on Novel futures across the board, but large differences remain. WhatIf-Bench reveals generalization to Novel futures as the core challenge, and offers a testbed toward arbitrary scenario analysis.

replace-cross ShlokaDhara: A Multi-Speaker Sanskrit Speech Corpus for Learning Meter-Aware Prosody in Text-to-Recitation

Authors: Harshal Kausadikar, Onkar Kishor Susladkar, Prathmesh Santosh Choudhari, Vedangi Deepak Deshpande, Ismini Lourentzou, Sparsh Mittal

Abstract: Sanskrit verse recitation is governed by $\textit{Chandas}$, a strict prosodic framework defined by rhythmic $\textit{Laghu}$ (light) and $\textit{Guru}$ (heavy) syllables. Because existing Sanskrit speech datasets prioritize ASR or conversational TTS, they lack the metrical fidelity required for authentic verse synthesis. We introduce $\textbf{$\textit{ShlokaDhara}$}$, a 43-hour, multi-speaker Sanskrit speech corpus capturing diverse meters, recitation styles, and melodic variations. We specifically focus on $\textit{Text-to-Recite}$, a synthesis task explicitly requiring both prosodic accuracy and metrical correctness. Benchmarking state-of-the-art TTS architectures via an automated UTMOS (neural MOS) predictor demonstrates that XTTS-v2 achieves the highest perceptual quality. We further validate the XTTS-v2 generated samples using two paradigms: subjective assessments by 16 Sanskrit experts and novel empirical metrics, specifically, syllable-level $\textit{Laghu-Guru}$ duration analysis to objectively quantify structural metrical awareness. Ultimately, this work establishes a robust dataset and evaluation benchmark for prosody-aware computational Sanskrit recitation. Generated Samples: https://shlokadhara.vercel.app/

URLs: https://shlokadhara.vercel.app/

replace-cross Disclosure Begets Disclosure: A Self-Reinforcing Privacy Loop in LLM Agents with Persistent Memory

Authors: Masahiro Kaneko, Hiromi Arai, Timothy Baldwin

Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents increasingly use persistent memory to personalize responses, and personal attributes in this memory pose a privacy risk if exposed. We ask how consulting memory during dialogue changes what users go on to say, and thus what the memory comes to reveal. In multi-session simulations, a simulated user with 24 personal attributes talks with a support agent that does or does not consult memory, and in both settings attributes are inferred from the full accumulated record. Across four support models, memory raises response quality but also empirical leakage, the share of attribute values correctly inferred from the record, by a margin that widens over sessions. The cause is a loop in ordinary, non-adversarial assistance. Memory-conditioned responses carry earlier values forward, often as presuppositions, and users affirm them or leave them uncorrected, so that cues and the agent's own guesses become recorded values. The loop reinforces itself, and removing the agent's mentions of an attribute lowers its later disclosure about twice as much as single-turn effects predict. Users cannot leave it on their own, since correcting a value discloses the attribute, declining to discuss it shifts later predictions toward sensitive values, and incorrect values left in place become entrenched. The loop is best weakened at the agent's response. Instructing the agent not to state or presuppose attributes brings leakage to about the no-memory level while keeping most of the quality gains, whereas filtering or anonymizing what users say or what is stored gives up much of them.

replace-cross Closed-Loop Dual-Reference Guidance for Accent Control in Cross-Lingual Voice Cloning

Authors: Yuanzhong Zheng, Wei Huang, Ming Ma, Kailun Jin, Youhai Jiang

Abstract: Zero-shot cross-lingual voice cloning often carries the reference speaker's accent into the target language. We propose closed-loop dual-reference guidance, which pairs the original reference with a reusable native anchor constructed by voice conversion (VC). The references initialize separate language-model (LM) states that guide a shared acoustic trajectory; each completed patch then updates both states, keeping their generated histories aligned with the emitted speech. Anchor construction runs once per speaker, and online synthesis uses a frozen TTS model without per-utterance VC. On 20 held-out French speakers, the fixed setting $\lambda^{\!*}=0.33$ reduces residual French LID mass by 73% versus direct cloning, at an ECAPA similarity cost of only $0.042$; relative to anchor-only cloning, it recovers $0.155$. The method achieves the lowest mean WER ($0.009$) and highest mean GOP ($4.750$) among the held-out test arms. In blinded preference tests, native-English listeners favor it over direct cloning on pronunciation naturalness, and over both anchor-only cloning and a TTS$\to$VC cascade on speaker identity. Online RTF is approximately 26% lower than the cascade's ($0.59$ vs. $0.80$), and the same setting reduces Spanish LID mass by 56% without re-tuning.

replace-cross TraceForge: Distilling with Your Own Thinking

Authors: Yan Wang

Abstract: Using an LLM to generate training data for another is among the most general ways to train one, and it remains common practice. Some frontier teams have closed that route for their own models: what comes back is an answer, or a short summary, without the detailed reasoning trace behind it. That is a little ironic: they were themselves built on distilling human knowledge, and distilling an open-source model is still entirely routine; the restriction applies to their own outputs alone. Recent work recovers the hidden reasoning by replaying encrypted blocks across models. It somehow works, but it breaks the providers' terms of service, so it is not something a product can be built on long-term. We built the alternative. We take the context and the teacher's answer as input, and generate the reasoning trace ourselves, in the style of the student's own model family. Training is two-stage, supervised and then adversarial: a generator learns to regenerate the family's own reasoning traces from their context and answer, and a critic learns how real a reasoning trace looks. Applied to a teacher's answer, the generator writes one in the family's style. An independent judge rates how likely traces are to be genuine on a scale from 0 to 1: ours average 0.68, real traces 0.66, and traces selected by the critic from four candidates 0.82. These are authenticity scores, not correctness rates. We test the idea on five benchmarks with everything else held fixed. On general tasks the five training corpora are about the same; on coding, the generated reasoning beats Fable 5's own thinking content. With the same 5,000 questions and the same teacher answers, HumanEval+ improves by 3.7 points and MBPP+ by 7.1 at the final checkpoint, and the MBPP+ lead holds at every checkpoint. We suspect that reasoning written in the student's family style is easier for it to learn from.

replace-cross RangeNav: Task-Aligned LiDAR Range-Image Pretraining for Robot Navigation

Authors: Samuel Foo Enze, Manthan Patel, Fan Yang, Linus Kramer, Pascal Roth, Marco Hutter, Cesar Cadena

Abstract: Autonomous mobile robots operating in dense crowds and cluttered sites require $360^\circ$ perception to react to surrounding hazards. While panoramic LiDAR offers full-surround coverage, learning representations within reinforcement learning (RL) is sample-inefficient under sparse rewards, and existing 3D pretrained point-cloud encoders are too memory-heavy for massively parallel RL simulation. We present RangeNav, a compact (183k parameters) LiDAR range-image encoder pretrained entirely on synthetic data. RangeNav uses anisotropic convolutions and circular padding to handle the extreme aspect ratio and azimuthal seam of range images. We pretrain the network using a navigation-aligned objective combining range reconstruction, semantic segmentation, and bird's-eye-view (BEV) prediction, then freeze it for policy learning. In simulation, adding semantic and BEV supervision increases success rates over a reconstruction-only baseline by an average of 32.03 and 23.73 percentage points (pp) in procedurally generated training and out-of-distribution realistic test environments, respectively. Zero-shot real-world deployments on a Unitree B2-W quadruped demonstrate robust navigation through dense crowds, low-light settings, and construction zones where limited-field-of-view depth policies struggle. Code and models will be open-sourced. Project website: https://rangenav.github.io/.

URLs: https://rangenav.github.io/.

replace-cross Tractable Inference with Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks

Authors: Juan Diego Toscano, Sascha Ranftl, Zhiwei Gao, Li-Lian Wang, George Em Karniadakis

Abstract: Machine-learning surrogates in computational science often require not only accurate predictions but also expectations, variances, conserved integrals, and normalization constants. Conventional neural networks, including Kolmogorov--Arnold networks (KANs), generally evaluate these quantities through Monte Carlo sampling or multidimensional quadrature, making repeated inference costly, particularly in high dimensions. We introduce KANs with Tractable Operators for Uncertainty and Constraints (TOUCKANs), whose finite-degree polynomial outer functions yield a decomposable sum--product representation. Under factorizing input measures, expectations and higher moments then reduce algebraically to deterministic recursions over one-dimensional leaf integrals. Independent and shared inner-block variants form a parameter-efficiency hierarchy, and a universal-approximation proof for its most restricted member extends to the complete class. The same deterministic operators allow conserved integrals and normalization to be imposed throughout training. Across uniform and Gaussian benchmarks through 50 dimensions, deterministic mean and variance errors remained below the median fixed-budget Monte Carlo errors in every case. TOUCKANs also conserved Cahn--Hilliard mass to machine precision and imposed normalization and orthogonality through deterministic moments, enabling joint computation of the lowest four Schr\"odinger eigenpairs in two, three, and five dimensions. TOUCKANs therefore connect KAN approximation with tractable probabilistic circuits, making statistical operators and integral constraints directly available within an expressive neural surrogate.

replace-cross Code Comprehensor: Hierarchical Documentation and Cross-Modal Retrieval for Large Repositories

Authors: Sujoy Roychowdhury, Joseph H. R. Isaac, Jiangning Gao, Martin Klitte, Diarmuid Corcoran

Abstract: Understanding large, complex, and rapidly evolving code bases remains a core challenge for software engineering at scale, hindering onboarding, maintenance, refactoring, and impact analysis. Providing this information to a Large-Language Model (LLM) is another hurdle, as the LLM must understand not only the required code method but also its associated methods in the grand scheme of the repository. We propose Code Comprehensor, a hybrid retrieval-reasoning framework combining (i) CodeGraph, a code dependency callgraph, (ii) CodeScribe, which uses code-aware LLMs to generate hierarchical documentation across repository call graphs, and (iii) CodeContrast, a CLIP-style dual-encoder model trained on CodeScribe output to learn aligned text-code embeddings. CodeContrast enables fast, semantically precise retrieval of relevant code-context neighborhoods, which serve as grounded evidence for an MCP-based LLM orchestrator answering domain-specific queries. Evaluation on OpenCV and a proprietary 5M-LOC repository shows that CodeScribe-derived supervision significantly improves cross-modal retrieval in text-to-code accuracy over baseline approaches. Richer textual supervision (hierarchical documentation, parent/sibling context, and LLM-generated synthetic questions) consistently enhances performance across both repositories. LLM-as-a-judge evaluation using the popular RAGAS of the Code Comprehensor output yields above 90% faithfulness scores.
